Deterministic thermostats and constrained degrees of freedom

Explore this chapter

  • Methods
  • Equations
  • Problem figures
  • Exact / reference solutions
  • Execution code

Compare thermostats on small harmonic/quartic systems and check effective degrees of freedom, momentum covariance and transient distributions under fixed linear constraints. Retain unmet statistical goals.

These additional validation supplements are managed separately from the common 22-example registry. Receipts cover independent ZIP extraction, tests and every fixed input within each group. Changes of settings, methods or step sizes do not add independent physical problems.

Routine supplement CI runs the supplement groups listed in the public registry with NumPy,SciPy and pytest. It checks versions,inputs and execution records for stored independent references and distinguishes their reuse from new reference computations. The existing common22-case suite,including quantum convergence,remains manual on explicit need; released artifacts,criteria and history are preserved.

Additional validation indexCode and tests / ZIPIndependent-extraction receipt
NH,chains,weak coupling and fixed-speed contrastsConstraints,centre-of-mass removal and canonical statistics

Download and execute the supplement

Run inside atlas-thermostat-constraint-controls-2026-11-25 from the ZIP. This command executes all 2 fixed cases in the supplement. The bundled results/ contains stored outputs. Rerun into a new output/ directory, and choose another fresh directory for subsequent runs.

python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python -m pytest -q
python run.py --input inputs.json --output output

Matplotlib is optional for redrawing saved figures; ordinary scientific execution does not require it.

NH,chains,weak coupling and fixed-speed contrasts

Compare distributions,fluctuations and correlations as well as mean temperature on the same two-mode systems.

Methods, equations, figures and references

Methods used

  • Nosé–Hoover thermostat
  • Nosé–Hoover chains
  • Berendsen weak-coupling thermostat
  • Direct velocity rescaling

Methods used by this fixed-input execution. Their guides describe applicable conditions and the checked scope.

Equations

This example’s formulation and conditions

Symmetrically compose exact frozen-variable bath subflows,two subcycles per half bath. Advance eta only in E subflows and compare short trajectories against independent full extendedODE references.

  • Hamiltonian systems and oscillators

    Fix unit masses,kBT1,frequencies1/1.7 and quartic0/.2. Compare NH1,NHC3,Berendsen and fixed-speed baths at two couplings/two steps with256canonical and128heated replicas.

  • Two-mode deterministic thermostat model

    Fix unit masses,kBT1,frequencies1/1.7 and quartic0/.2. Compare NH1,NHC3,Berendsen and fixed-speed baths at two couplings/two steps with256canonical and128heated replicas.

This problem’s code and validation status

Exact / reference solutions

References and checks for this execution

Separately test physical reversal,Hext derivative,phi1,local weak-coupling inverses and noninjective rescaling. Retain four distributions,fluctuations,signed correlations and unresolved DKW precision at time20.

  • Same-problem reference · Semi-analytic reference

    NH,chains,weak coupling and fixed-speed contrasts

    Same two-mode quartic system plus a harmonic control; short-time and finite-sample deterministic-bath comparisons.

Assumptions, units and boundaries

Fix unit masses,kBT1,frequencies1/1.7 and quartic0/.2. Compare NH1,NHC3,Berendsen and fixed-speed baths at two couplings/two steps with256canonical and128heated replicas.

Editorial clarification

This authored splitting is not a full LAMMPS/MKT reproduction. Time20does not prove mixing/equilibration; correct mean temperature alone does not establish canonical sampling or general ergodicity. Study targets compare canonical moments and correlations across different baths and finite-time canonical/heated ensembles. Retained unmet outcomes must be read by bath and initial ensemble, separately from mandatory implementation and reproduction checks.

Adopted numerical method

Symmetrically compose exact frozen-variable bath subflows,two subcycles per half bath. Advance eta only in E subflows and compare short trajectories against independent full extendedODE references.

p˙=−∇U−ξ1p,ξ˙1=(2K−fT)/Q1−ξ2ξ1\dot p=-\nabla U-\xi_1p,\quad\dot\xi_1=(2K-fT)/Q_1-\xi_2\xi_1p˙​=−∇U−ξ1​p,ξ˙​1​=(2K−fT)/Q1​−ξ2​ξ1​
Hext=H+∑jQjξj2/2+fTη1+T∑j>1ηjH_{ext}=H+\sum_jQ_j\xi_j^2/2+fT\eta_1+T\sum_{j>1}\eta_jHext​=H+j∑​Qj​ξj2​/2+fTη1​+Tj>1∑​ηj​

References and measured quantities

Separately test physical reversal,Hext derivative,phi1,local weak-coupling inverses and noninjective rescaling. Retain four distributions,fluctuations,signed correlations and unresolved DKW precision at time20.

Verification limits and failures

This authored splitting is not a full LAMMPS/MKT reproduction. Time20does not prove mixing/equilibration; correct mean temperature alone does not establish canonical sampling or general ergodicity.

    Constraints,centre-of-mass removal and canonical statistics

    These fixed linear constraints do not cover nonlinear molecular constraints,Fixman corrections or transport coefficients. Redundant systems with independent seeds need not have identical paths; CSVR dynamical correlations are empirical.

Assumptions, units and boundaries

Use three1Dparticles of masses1/2/3 in one common harmonic potential. No constraints,COM,COM-relative and a redundant-row equivalent have f3/2/1/1,each with1,024replicas.

Editorial clarification

These fixed linear constraints do not cover nonlinear molecular constraints,Fixman corrections or transport coefficients. Redundant systems with independent seeds need not have identical paths; CSVR dynamical correlations are empirical.

Adopted numerical method

Apply tangent-space Langevin/CSVR in OBABO form. Build finite-time maps and noise covariance from canonical initial data; compute continuous references and stationary discrete Lyapunov solutions separately.

Cq=0,CM−1p=0,f=3−rank(C)Cq=0,\quad CM^{-1}p=0,\quad f=3-\mathrm{rank}(C)Cq=0,CM−1p=0,f=3−rank(C)
Cov(p)=T(M−CT(CM−1CT)+C)\mathrm{Cov}(p)=T(M-C^T(CM^{-1}C^T)^+C)Cov(p)=T(M−CT(CM−1CT)+C)

References and measured quantities

Check independent Fraction rank/covariance,signedf1 baths,the valid projectedOU identity and a wrong-ambientDOF counterexample. Retain220CDF families,signed two-time products and correct/incorrect temperature denominators.

Verification limits and failures

These fixed linear constraints do not cover nonlinear molecular constraints,Fixman corrections or transport coefficients. Redundant systems with independent seeds need not have identical paths; CSVR dynamical correlations are empirical.

Equations

  • Canonical momentum under fixed linear constraints

    These fixed linear constraints do not cover nonlinear molecular constraints,Fixman corrections or transport coefficients. Redundant systems with independent seeds need not have identical paths; CSVR dynamical correlations are empirical. Use three1Dparticles of masses1/2/3 in one common harmonic potential. No constraints,COM,COM-relative and a redundant-row equivalent have f3/2/1/1,each with1,024replicas.

    Check independent Fraction rank/covariance,signedf1 baths,the valid projectedOU identity and a wrong-ambientDOF counterexample. Retain220CDF families,signed two-time products and correct/incorrect temperature denominators.

  • Constraints,centre-of-mass removal and canonical statistics

    Same-problem reference

    Semi-analytic reference

    Semi-analytic reference Use three1Dparticles of masses1/2/3 in one common harmonic potential. No constraints,COM,COM-relative and a redundant-row equivalent have f3/2/1/1,each with1,024replicas. Dimensionless; masses 1/2/3 and kBT=1. Fixed linear constraints Cq=0 and CM⁻¹p=0; redundant rows are compared on the same tangent space. These fixed linear constraints do not cover nonlinear molecular constraints,Fixman corrections or transport coefficients. Redundant systems with independent seeds need not have identical paths; CSVR dynamical correlations are empirical.

  • NH,chains,weak coupling and fixed-speed contrasts

    Semi-analytic reference

    Semi-analytic reference Fix unit masses,kBT1,frequencies1/1.7 and quartic0/.2. Compare NH1,NHC3,Berendsen and fixed-speed baths at two couplings/two steps with256canonical and128heated replicas. Dimensionless, unit masses, kBT=1, frequencies 1/1.7; Q1=2tau², Qj=tau². Two-mode initial-value problem without spatial boundaries; fixed canonical/heated ensembles.

  • Anharmonic CSVR versus Langevin: distribution checks against Boltzmann quadrature — references and comparison conditions

    Compare thermostats on small harmonic/quartic systems and check effective degrees of freedom, momentum covariance and transient distributions under fixed linear constraints. Retain unmet statistical goals. Independently integrate the anharmonic Boltzmann distribution and compare finite-step CSVR/Langevin distributions and moments, not exact stochastic trajectories.
